<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let $`V(x)`$ count the distinct values of Euler's totient function up to <i>x</i>. We give an explicit asymptotic equivalent for $`V(x)`$. Its coefficient is a uniform limit of functions defined from finite arithmetic data. We also prove that $`V(cx)/V(x)\to c`$ as $`x\to\infty`$ for every fixed <i>c</i> &gt; 0, answering a question of Erdős and Hall.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For sufficiently large positive integers $H$, there are nonnegative functions $A_H(1;\cdot):[0,1)\to\mathbb R$, given by the finite arithmetic formula <a href="#eq:AH" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:AH">[eq:AH]</a> below, which converge uniformly as $H\to\infty$ to a function $A(1;\cdot)$ satisfying $$0<\inf_{0\le s<1}A(1;s) \le\sup_{0\le s<1}A(1;s)<\infty.$$ With the parameters <a href="#eq:renewal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:renewal">[eq:renewal]</a>–<a href="#eq:Gj" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:Gj">[eq:Gj]</a>, $$V(x)\sim\frac{x}{\log x}\,G_m A(1;\theta).$$ Moreover, for every fixed real $c>0$, $$\frac{V(cx)}{V(x)}\longrightarrow c.$$
  - Explanation: The explicit asymptotic equivalent uses a phase-dependent arithmetic coefficient obtained by uniform convergence. Fixed dilation has its own comparison argument.
- **C2** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-companion)): For each fixed positive integer $k$, the functions $A_H(f_k;s)$ converge uniformly for $0\le s<1$, and $$N_k(x)=\frac{x}{\log x}\,G_m\bigl(A(f_k;\theta)+o(1)\bigr).$$ There are two possibilities.* 1. *If some $d\in\mathcal V$ satisfies $\ell(d)>kd$, then $$\inf_{0\le s<1}A(f_k;s)>0,\qquad N_k(x)\sim\frac{x}{\log x}\,G_m A(f_k;\theta) \asymp_k V(x).$$* 2. *If no such $d$ exists, then $N_k(x)=0$ for every $x>0$, and $A(f_k;s)=0$ for every $s\in[0,1)$.* *The first alternative holds for $k=1,2$.
  - Explanation: Least-preimage interval counts have a weighted asymptotic. A seed ratio exceeding the fixed interval index distinguishes a positive coefficient from an identically zero count.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-basic)): There is a set $E_x\subseteq\mathcal V\cap[1,x]$ with $$\|E_x\|\le\{\varepsilon_H+o_{x;H}(1)\}V(x)$$ such that every preimage of every $v\in(\mathcal V\cap[1,x])\setminus E_x$ is $p_0\cdots p_La$ for a basic witness. One may take $$\varepsilon_H\ll \mathrm e^{-cP}+K_H\exp\{-\exp(cP\rho^{-P})\}.$$ For every basic witness, putting $w=p_{R+1}\cdots p_La$, we have $$\label{eq:cofactor-size} \begin{gathered} p_1\cdots p_La\le\exp((\log x)^{0.8}),\\ \log w\le H\exp(0.62b_R)+\exp(2\widetilde b_L), \qquad w<p_R. \end{gathered}$$ In particular $w$ is bounded in terms of $H$ alone, uniformly in the phase.
  - Explanation: Outside an asymptotically negligible exceptional set, every preimage admits the specified prime prefix and bounded arithmetic tail.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-simplex)): Let $1\le j\le m$. The true simplex $S_j(\boldsymbol1)$ has volume $G_j$. Under its uniform volume measure the variables $W_i=g_is_i/B$ are uniform on $\{W_i\ge0:\sum_{i=0}^jW_i=1\}$. Moreover, $$\operatorname{Vol}S_j(\boldsymbol\beta) \le G_j\prod_{r<j}\beta_r^{j-r}.$$ Within $S_j(\boldsymbol\beta)$, a specified slack $s_t$ in an interval of length $D$ occupies at most a fraction $jg_tD/B$ of the volume. A specified coordinate $u_t$, $1\le t\le j$, in such an interval occupies at most $jg_t\beta_tD/B$.
  - Explanation: Slack coordinates give the exact simplex volume and its uniform measure, together with enlargement and slice bounds.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-concentration)): Fix $\eta>0$. There are constants $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, if $Q$ is an integer with $0\le Q<m$, $j=m-Q$, $1\le i\le j$, and $h_i\ge C_\eta(Q+1)$, then uniform volume measure on $S_j(\boldsymbol1)$ satisfies $$\Pr(\|u_i/b_i-1\|>\eta)\le C_\eta\mathrm e^{-c_\eta h_i}.$$ Also, for $T\ge0$, $$\Pr(u_j\ge T)=(1-g_jT/B)_+^j\le\mathrm e^{-jg_jT/B}.$$
  - Explanation: Uniform simplex measure concentrates around the characteristic coordinate bands once the distance from the terminal cut is sufficiently large.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-boxes)): Let $0\le j\le L$. Sum over primes $p_1,\ldots,p_j$ whose coordinates satisfy $$0.9\widetilde b_i\le u_i\le1.1\widetilde b_i,\qquad (u_1,\ldots,u_j)\in S_j(\boldsymbol\xi).$$ Then $$\label{eq:projected-mass} \sum_{p_1,\ldots,p_j}\prod_{i=1}^j\frac1{p_i-1}\ll G_m.$$ Every unit box meeting this region lies in $S_j(\boldsymbol\beta)$, where, with a sufficiently large absolute constant, $$\label{eq:box-enlargement} \beta_t=1+C\bigl(\mathrm e^{-h_t/40}+h_t^2\rho^{h_t}\bigr) \quad(0\le t<j),\qquad \beta_j=1.$$ This enlargement has bounded volume cost. Its coordinate scaling factors through $R$ differ from $1$ by $O(\mathrm e^{-H/40}+H^2\rho^H)$.* *There is also the following shell estimate in $L$ coordinates. Fix $A>0$. Among unit boxes meeting the displayed region, retain those with a point satisfying, for some $i\le R$, at least one of these conditions:* 1. *$i\ge1$ and $u_i$ is within $A$ of a coarse band endpoint;* 2. *the absolute value of $\xi_i u_i-\sum_{r=i+1}^L a_{r-i}u_r$ is at most $Ah_i^2$;* 3. *$\sum_{r=i+1}^L a_{r-i}u_r\ge(1-h_i^{-4})u_i$.* *The total volume of these boxes, and their total reciprocal-prime weight, is $$\label{eq:boundary-mass} O_A(H^{-2}G_m).$$ The same estimate holds when any subset of coordinates uses reciprocal-prime measure and the others use Lebesgue measure. For conditions (i)–(ii) alone, or for the region lost by replacing the prefix coefficients $\xi_i$, $0\le i\le R$, with $1$, the stronger bound is $$O_A\left(G_m\sum_{h\ge H} (h\mathrm e^{-h/40}+h^3\rho^h)\right).$$
  - Explanation: Unit boxes convert reciprocal-prime sums into controlled simplex volumes and bound the loss near coordinate bands and slack faces.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-smooth-tail)): Uniformly for $Y\ge\exp(\exp2)$ and $Z\ge0$, $$\sum_{P^+(a)\le Y}\frac{a^{1/\log Y}}{\varphi(a)}\ll\log Y, \qquad \sum_{\substack{P^+(a)\le Y\\\log a>Z}}\frac1{\varphi(a)} \ll(\log Y)\exp(-Z/\log Y).$$ In particular, with $Y=\exp\exp(1.1\widetilde b_L)$, $$\label{eq:smooth-cost} \sum_{P^+(a)\le Y}\frac1{\varphi(a)}\le K_H,\qquad \sum_{\substack{P^+(a)\le Y\\\log a>\exp(2\widetilde b_L)}} \frac1{\varphi(a)} \le K_H\exp\{-\exp(cP\rho^{-P})\}.$$
  - Explanation: An Euler product and Rankin bound control the reciprocal totient mass of a smooth cofactor and its large-size tail.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-discard)): For $t=x$ and $t=x/c$, with the same $x$-dependent parameters, $$\|\mathcal D(t)\| \le\{\varepsilon_H+o_{x;H}(1)\}\frac{xG_m}{\log x}.$$ One may take $\varepsilon_H\ll K_HH^{-2}$. For all the witnesses in <a href="#eq:good-conditions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:good-conditions">[eq:good-conditions]</a> we also have $$\label{eq:tail-cutoffs} P^+(D_i)<z_i,\qquad S_i<z_i,\qquad c h_i^{-20}\le b_{i+J_i}/b_i\le C h_i^{-18}.$$
  - Explanation: Discarded tuples have negligible mass at both endpoints with all parameters formed from the same argument.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sieve)): There are absolute constants $C$ and $y_0$ with the following property. Let $y\ge y_0$, put $B_y=\log_2 y$, and let $1\le A,A'\le y$ be integers. Consider either the two forms $$n,\quad An+1,$$ or the three forms $$n,\quad An+1,\quad A'n+1,\qquad A\ne A'.$$ Write $k=2$ or $3$, respectively, and let $\mathcal P$ be the set of positive integers at which all the indicated forms are prime. Then, uniformly for $X\ge3$, $$\label{eq:sieve-count} \#(\mathcal P\cap[1,X]) \le C\frac{X B_y^k}{(\log X)^k}.$$ Moreover, uniformly for $3\le U\le Y$, $$\label{eq:sieve-reciprocal} \sum_{\substack{n\in\mathcal P\\U\le n\le Y}}\frac1n \le C\frac{B_y^k}{(\log U)^{k-1}}.$$ The coefficients need not be bounded in terms of $X$ or $U$.
  - Explanation: Uniform upper sieve estimates bound simultaneous primality of the stated distinct linear forms and their reciprocal input sum.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-comparison)): There are absolute constants $C$ and $y_0$ such that the following holds. Let $y\ge y_0$, put $B_y=\log_2 y$, and let $b$ be an integer with $1\le b\le B_y$. Suppose that real cutoffs satisfy $$Y_0=y,\qquad \mathrm e^\mathrm e\le S\le Y_b, \qquad Y_{j+1}<U_j<Y_j\quad(0\le j<b).$$ Put $$\nu_j=\frac{\log_2 Y_j}{B_y}\quad(0\le j\le b), \qquad \mu_j=\frac{\log_2 U_j}{B_y}\quad(0\le j<b), \qquad \delta=\sqrt{\frac{\log_2 S}{B_y}}.$$ Fix a positive integer $D$ with $P^+(D)\le Y_b$ and a real $r\ge1$. Let $N$ count the ordered tuples $$(p_0,\ldots,p_{b-1},q_0,\ldots,q_{b-1},D')$$ in which $D'$ is a positive integer and the following conditions hold:* 1. *The $p_j,q_j$ are $S$-normal primes, $p_j\ne q_j$, and $$U_j\le P^+(p_j-1),P^+(q_j-1)\le Y_j \qquad(0\le j<b).$$* 2. *$P^+(D')\le Y_b$ and $$D\prod_{j=0}^{b-1}(p_j-1) =D'\prod_{j=0}^{b-1}(q_j-1)\le\frac yr.$$* 3. *The common product in <span class="upright">(ii)</span> is squarefree on primes greater than $Y_b$.* 4. *The part of $p_0-1$ supported above $Y_1$ exceeds $\sqrt y$.* *Then $$\label{eq:comparison} \begin{split} N\le{}&\frac{y}{rD}(CB_y^6)^b(b+1)^{\Omega(D)} (\log Y_b)^{1+3b\log(b(b+1))}\\ &\quad\times (\log y)^{-2+\sum_{j=1}^{b-1}a_j\nu_j+E_b}, \end{split}$$ where $$\label{eq:comparison-error} E_b=2\sum_{j=1}^{b-1}(\nu_j-\mu_j) +\delta\sum_{i=2}^b(i\log i+i).$$ Here $a_j$ is as defined in <a href="#eq:aj" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:aj">[eq:aj]</a>. Empty sums are zero; in particular, when $b=1$ the cutoff in <span class="upright">(iv)</span> is $Y_1=Y_b$.
  - Explanation: Layered factor allocations bound ordered colliding tuples under normality, cutoff separation and squarefreeness of the whole common product above the terminal cutoff.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-rem-comparison-uniformity)): The explicit error in <a href="#eq:comparison-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:comparison-error">[eq:comparison-error]</a> satisfies $$E_b=2\sum_{j=1}^{b-1}(\nu_j-\mu_j) +O\bigl(\delta b^2\log(2b)\bigr),$$ and the smooth-factor exponent is $O(b\log(2b))$. Thus all dependence on a growing $b$ is displayed in <a href="#eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:comparison">[eq:comparison]</a>. No restriction such as $r\le y^{1/10}$ or $D\le y^{1/100}$ is needed: the large top part supplies the lower bound for the actual sieve endpoint.
  - Explanation: The comparison estimate displays its dependence on the growing layer count and does not need the auxiliary size restrictions discussed here.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-collisions)): Fix $c>1$, and form all parameters from $x$, with $H$ fixed before $x$ tends to infinity. For $t=x$ or $t=x/c$, let $$K(t)=\#\{(\tau,\sigma)\in\mathcal G(t)^2: \tau\ne\sigma,\ F(\tau)=F(\sigma)\}.$$ There is a quantity $\epsilon_H\to0$, independent of the phase, such that $$K(t)\le\bigl(\epsilon_H+o_{x;H}(1)\bigr) \frac{xG_m}{\log x}.$$ The pairs counted here are ordered pairs of distinct tuples.
  - Explanation: Ordered pairs of distinct good tuples with equal values have negligible mass on the distinct-totient counting scale.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-map)): Let $T$ and $W$ be finite sets, let $F:T\to W$, and write $T=G\sqcup D$. Suppose that every element of $W\setminus E$ is in $F(T)$, where $E\subset W$. For $w\in W$ put $r_w=\|G\cap F^{-1}(w)\|$, and let $$K=\sum_{w\in W}r_w(r_w-1).$$ Then $$-\|E\|\le \|T\|-\|W\|\le \|D\|+K/2.$$ Moreover, with $E^*=E\cup F(D)\cup\{w:r_w\ge2\}$, one has $$\|E^*\|\le\|E\|+\|D\|+K/2,\qquad \|F^{-1}(E^*)\|\le\|E\|+2\|D\|+K.$$ The restriction of $F$ to $T\setminus F^{-1}(E^*)$ is a bijection onto $W\setminus E^*$.
  - Explanation: Finite-map fiber counts control the discrepancy between tuple and value counts, including the tuples above exceptional values.
- **C14** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-unique-prefix)): For $t=x$ or $t=x/c$, with the same parameters formed from $x$, $$\label{eq:tuple-cardinality} \bigl\|\,\|\mathcal T(t)\|-V(t)\,\bigr\| \le\bigl(\epsilon_H+o_{x;H}(1)\bigr)\frac{xG_m}{\log x}, \qquad\epsilon_H\longrightarrow0.$$ There is a set $E^*(t)\subset\mathcal V\cap[1,t]$ for which both $\|E^*(t)\|$ and the number of tuples in $\mathcal T(t)$ with values in $E^*(t)$ are at most $(\epsilon_H+o_{x;H}(1))xG_m/\log x$, after enlarging $\epsilon_H$. For every $v\in(\mathcal V\cap[1,t])\setminus E^*(t)$ there is a unique tuple $(p_0,\ldots,p_R,d)\in\mathcal T(t)$ of value $v$, and every preimage of $v$ has this prefix and a remaining factor of totient $d$. In particular, $$\label{eq:least-factorization} \ell(v)=p_0\cdots p_R\ell(d).$$
  - Explanation: Almost every value has a unique shortened tuple. Every complete preimage shares that prefix, which yields the exact least-preimage factorization.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-mass)): For each fixed $c>1$, both $t=x$ and $t=x/c$ satisfy $$V(t)=\frac{t}{\log x} \bigl(M_1(x;H)+E_H(t;x)G_m\bigr), \qquad \lim_{H\to\infty}\limsup_{x\to\infty} \max_{t\in\{x,x/c\}}\|E_H(t;x)\|=0. \label{eq:mass-count}$$ All parameters in the two formulas are formed from the same $x$.
  - Explanation: Counting the largest prime expresses both endpoint counts through a common reciprocal mass with a vanishing iterated-limit error.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-fixed-scaling)): For every fixed real $c>0$, $$\frac{V(cx)}{V(x)}\longrightarrow c.$$
  - Explanation: The distinct-totient count has the stated fixed-factor dilation limit for each positive real multiplier.
- **C17** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-tail-data)): For each sufficiently large fixed $H$, the union of all $\mathcal W_{H,s}(d)$, over $0\le s<1$ and all $d$, is finite. All its prime and integer coordinates, and all its totient values, have explicit bounds depending only on $H$. Uniformly over these data, $$0\le D(h,\eta)\ll_H\log(2h)\qquad(h\ge H). \label{eq:tail-D-bound}$$
  - Explanation: For a fixed sufficiently large tail cut, all phases share a finite universe of arithmetic witnesses with explicitly bounded coordinates.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tail-volume)): There are numbers $\delta_H\to0$ such that, uniformly for $f:[1,\infty)\to[0,1]$, $$\limsup_{x\to\infty} \left\|\frac{M_f(x;H)}{G_m} -\frac1{G_m}\sum_{d:\mathcal W_{H,\theta}(d)\ne\varnothing} \frac{f(\ell(d)/d)}d \operatorname{Vol}_R \bigcup_{\eta\in\mathcal W_{H,\theta}(d)}\mathcal K_\eta \right\|\le\delta_H. \label{eq:mass-volume}$$ Moreover, for every nonempty finite $T\subseteq\mathcal W_{H,\theta}(d)$, $$\operatorname{Vol}_R\bigcap_{\eta\in T}\mathcal K_\eta =G_R\left(1-\frac1B\sum_{h=H}^m g_{m-h} \max_{\eta\in T}D(h,\eta)\right)_+^R. \label{eq:intersection-volume}$$ Here $z_+=\max(z,0)$.
  - Explanation: The mass is approximated by the volume of a witness union. Intersections are translated simplex volumes with coordinatewise maximum thresholds.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-tail-limit)): There are numbers $\varepsilon_H\to0$ such that, for every $f:[1,\infty)\to[0,1]$, $$\limsup_{x\to\infty} \left\|\frac{M_f(x;H)}{G_m}-A_H(f;\theta(x))\right\| \le\varepsilon_H. \label{eq:uniform-approximation}$$ The bound is independent of $f$ and of phase. Each $A_H(f;s)$ is a finite sum involving values of $f$ at explicitly bounded rational arguments and absolutely convergent series in its exponential weights, and $A_H(f;s)\ge0$.
  - Explanation: Normalized reciprocal masses are approximated uniformly in phase and bounded nonnegative weight by the finite arithmetic coefficient.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-coefficient-convergence)): Fix $f:[1,\infty)\to[0,1]$. Suppose a real function $C_f(x)$, independent of $H$, and numbers $\eta_H\to0$ satisfy $$\limsup_{x\to\infty} \left\|C_f(x)-\frac{M_f(x;H)}{G_m}\right\|\le\eta_H. \label{eq:common-comparison}$$ Then $A_H(f;s)$ converges uniformly for $0\le s<1$ to the limit $A(f;s)$ defined in Section <a href="#sec:statements" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:statements">2</a>, and $$C_f(x)=A(f;\theta(x))+o(1). \label{eq:comparison-limit}$$ If additionally $c_-\le C_f(x)\le c_+$ for all sufficiently large $x$, then $c_-\le A(f;s)\le c_+$ for every $s\in[0,1)$.
  - Explanation: An independent comparison function with vanishing error makes the coefficient approximants uniformly Cauchy across phases.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-endpoint)): For each sufficiently large fixed $H$, as $x$ tends to infinity, $$\label{eq:weighted-mass} \left\|N_k(x)-\frac{x}{\log x}M_{f_k}(x;H)\right\| \leq\bigl(\epsilon_{H,k}+o_{x\to\infty;H,k}(1)\bigr) \frac{xG_m}{\log x},$$ where $\epsilon_{H,k}\geq0$ tends to zero as $H\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Counting the permitted largest-prime interval gives an additive weighted mass approximation that also handles zero-weight boundaries.
- **C22** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-fiber-propagation)): Fix a totient $d$, and write $\varphi^{-1}(d)=\{d_1,\ldots,d_\kappa\}$. There are constants $\eta_d>0$ and $x_d$ such that, for every $x\geq x_d$, at least $\eta_dV(x)$ distinct totients $v\leq x$ have the form $$v=d\varphi(b),\qquad \varphi^{-1}(v)=\{bd_1,\ldots,bd_\kappa\}$$ for some positive integer $b$. In particular, these values satisfy $\ell(v)/v\geq\ell(d)/d$.
  - Explanation: A fixed complete inverse fiber propagates to a positive proportion of distinct totients and preserves its least-preimage ratio lower bound.
- **C23** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ratio-tightness)): For every $\varepsilon>0$, there are $C<\infty$ and $x_0$ such that $$\#\{v\in\mathcal V:v\leq x,\ \ell(v)/v>C\} \leq\varepsilon V(x)\qquad(x\geq x_0).$$
  - Explanation: The least-preimage ratio is bounded outside an arbitrarily small asymptotic proportion of totient values.
- **C24** (classification, [source](#stmt-rem-least-preimage-scope)): The positive alternative occurs for $k=1$ and $k=2$. Ford gives $d=2^{18}\cdot257$ as a totient all of whose preimages are divisible by $8$ (Ford 1998, sec. 7.3, p. 39) [[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)]. Each such preimage $n$ is even and has an odd prime factor: a power of $2$ would have a power of $2$ as its totient. Thus $n/\varphi(n)=\prod_{p\mid n}p/(p-1)>2$, and $\ell(d)>2d$. We do not determine all integers $k$ for which the positive alternative occurs. Its occurrence for every integer $k\geq1$ is equivalent to the unboundedness of $\ell(d)/d$ over totients $d$. Neither that unboundedness nor the complete classification of $k$ is established here.
  - Explanation: The displayed seed supplies the positive alternatives stated here. The classification of all interval indices and unboundedness of least-preimage ratios remain unresolved.

```json
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boxes",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le j\\le L$. Sum over primes $p_1,\\ldots,p_j$ whose coordinates satisfy $$0.9\\widetilde b_i\\le u_i\\le1.1\\widetilde b_i,\\qquad\n (u_1,\\ldots,u_j)\\in S_j(\\boldsymbol\\xi).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:projected-mass}\n \\sum_{p_1,\\ldots,p_j}\\prod_{i=1}^j\\frac1{p_i-1}\\ll G_m.$$ Every unit box meeting this region lies in $S_j(\\boldsymbol\\beta)$, where, with a sufficiently large absolute constant, $$\\label{eq:box-enlargement}\n \\beta_t=1+C\\bigl(\\mathrm e^{-h_t/40}+h_t^2\\rho^{h_t}\\bigr)\n \\quad(0\\le t<j),\\qquad \\beta_j=1.$$ This enlargement has bounded volume cost. Its coordinate scaling factors through $R$ differ from $1$ by $O(\\mathrm e^{-H/40}+H^2\\rho^H)$.*\n\n*There is also the following shell estimate in $L$ coordinates. Fix $A>0$. Among unit boxes meeting the displayed region, retain those with a point satisfying, for some $i\\le R$, at least one of these conditions:*\n\n1.  *$i\\ge1$ and $u_i$ is within $A$ of a coarse band endpoint;*\n\n2.  *the absolute value of $\\xi_i u_i-\\sum_{r=i+1}^L a_{r-i}u_r$ is at most $Ah_i^2$;*\n\n3.  *$\\sum_{r=i+1}^L a_{r-i}u_r\\ge(1-h_i^{-4})u_i$.*\n\n*The total volume of these boxes, and their total reciprocal-prime weight, is $$\\label{eq:boundary-mass}\n O_A(H^{-2}G_m).$$ The same estimate holds when any subset of coordinates uses reciprocal-prime measure and the others use Lebesgue measure. For conditions (i)–(ii) alone, or for the region lost by replacing the prefix coefficients $\\xi_i$, $0\\le i\\le R$, with $1$, the stronger bound is $$O_A\\left(G_m\\sum_{h\\ge H}\n       (h\\mathrm e^{-h/40}+h^3\\rho^h)\\right).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-024.1"
      },

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sieve",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C$ and $y_0$ with the following property. Let $y\\ge y_0$, put $B_y=\\log_2 y$, and let $1\\le A,A'\\le y$ be integers. Then, uniformly for $X\\ge3$, $$\\label{eq:sieve-count}\n \\#(\\mathcal P\\cap[1,X])\n \\le C\\frac{X B_y^k}{(\\log X)^k}.$$ Moreover, uniformly for $3\\le U\\le Y$, $$\\label{eq:sieve-reciprocal}\n \\sum_{\\substack{n\\in\\mathcal P\\\\U\\le n\\le Y}}\\frac1n\n \\le C\\frac{B_y^k}{(\\log U)^{k-1}}.$$ The coefficients need not be bounded in terms of $X$ or $U$.",

      },
      "evidence": [

        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C$ and $y_0$ such that the following holds. Let $y\\ge y_0$, put $B_y=\\log_2 y$, and let $b$ be an integer with $1\\le b\\le B_y$. Suppose that real cutoffs satisfy $$Y_0=y,\\qquad \\mathrm e^\\mathrm e\\le S\\le Y_b,\n \\qquad Y_{j+1}<U_j<Y_j\\quad(0\\le j<b).$$ Put $$\\nu_j=\\frac{\\log_2 Y_j}{B_y}\\quad(0\\le j\\le b),\n \\qquad\n \\mu_j=\\frac{\\log_2 U_j}{B_y}\\quad(0\\le j<b),\n \\qquad\n \\delta=\\sqrt{\\frac{\\log_2 S}{B_y}}.$$ Fix a positive integer $D$ with $P^+(D)\\le Y_b$ and a real $r\\ge1$. Let $N$ count the ordered tuples $$(p_0,\\ldots,p_{b-1},q_0,\\ldots,q_{b-1},D')$$ in which $D'$ is a positive integer and the following conditions hold:*\n\n1.  *The $p_j,q_j$ are $S$-normal primes, $p_j\\ne q_j$, and $$U_j\\le P^+(p_j-1),P^+(q_j-1)\\le Y_j\n      \\qquad(0\\le j<b).$$*\n\n2.  *$P^+(D')\\le Y_b$ and $$D\\prod_{j=0}^{b-1}(p_j-1)\n      =D'\\prod_{j=0}^{b-1}(q_j-1)\\le\\frac yr.$$*\n\n3.  *The common product in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> is squarefree on primes greater than $Y_b$.*\n\n4.  *The part of $p_0-1$ supported above $Y_1$ exceeds $\\sqrt y$.*\n\n\n\n*Then $$\\label{eq:comparison}\n \\begin{split}\n N\\le{}&\\frac{y}{rD}(CB_y^6)^b(b+1)^{\\Omega(D)}\n       (\\log Y_b)^{1+3b\\log(b(b+1))}\\\\\n &\\quad\\times\n (\\log y)^{-2+\\sum_{j=1}^{b-1}a_j\\nu_j+E_b},\n \\end{split}$$ where $$\\label{eq:comparison-error}\n E_b=2\\sum_{j=1}^{b-1}(\\nu_j-\\mu_j)\n      +\\delta\\sum_{i=2}^b(i\\log i+i).$$ Here $a_j$ is as defined in <a href=\"#eq:aj\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:aj\">[eq:aj]</a>. Empty sums are zero; in particular, when $b=1$ the cutoff in <span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> is $Y_1=Y_b$.",

      },

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-024.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-unique-prefix",
        "exact_quote": "For $t=x$ or $t=x/c$, with the same parameters formed from $x$, $$\\label{eq:tuple-cardinality}\n \\bigl|\\,|\\mathcal T(t)|-V(t)\\,\\bigr|\n       \\le\\bigl(\\epsilon_H+o_{x;H}(1)\\bigr)\\frac{xG_m}{\\log x},\n \\qquad\\epsilon_H\\longrightarrow0.$$ There is a set $E^*(t)\\subset\\mathcal V\\cap[1,t]$ for which both $|E^*(t)|$ and the number of tuples in $\\mathcal T(t)$ with values in $E^*(t)$ are at most $(\\epsilon_H+o_{x;H}(1))xG_m/\\log x$, after enlarging $\\epsilon_H$. For every $v\\in(\\mathcal V\\cap[1,t])\\setminus E^*(t)$ there is a unique tuple $(p_0,\\ldots,p_R,d)\\in\\mathcal T(t)$ of value $v$, and every preimage of $v$ has this prefix and a remaining factor of totient $d$. In particular, $$\\label{eq:least-factorization}\n \\ell(v)=p_0\\cdots p_R\\ell(d).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-024.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-unique-prefix",
          "exact_quote": "For $t=x$ or $t=x/c$, with the same parameters formed from $x$,",

        }

      "exact_statement": "For $t=x$ or $t=x/c$, with the same parameters formed from $x$, $$\\label{eq:tuple-cardinality}\n \\bigl|\\,|\\mathcal T(t)|-V(t)\\,\\bigr|\n       \\le\\bigl(\\epsilon_H+o_{x;H}(1)\\bigr)\\frac{xG_m}{\\log x},\n \\qquad\\epsilon_H\\longrightarrow0.$$ There is a set $E^*(t)\\subset\\mathcal V\\cap[1,t]$ for which both $|E^*(t)|$ and the number of tuples in $\\mathcal T(t)$ with values in $E^*(t)$ are at most $(\\epsilon_H+o_{x;H}(1))xG_m/\\log x$, after enlarging $\\epsilon_H$. <!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Euler totient** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Euler’s totient function $\varphi(n)$ counts the integers in $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ that are relatively prime to $n$.
- **Distinct totient values and their count** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We write $$\mathcal V=\{\varphi(n):n\ge1\},\qquad V(x)=\#\{v\in\mathcal V:v\le x\}.$$
- **Iterated logarithms** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Throughout the paper logarithms are natural, and $\log_j$ denotes the $j$-fold iterated logarithm.
- **Least preimage** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For $v\in\mathcal V$, let $$\ell(v)=\min\{n\ge1:\varphi(n)=v\}.$$
- **Renewal coefficients** ([source](#eq:aj)): For $j\ge1$, put $$\label{eq:aj} a_j=(j+1)\log(j+1)-j\log j-1 =\int_j^{j+1}\log t\,\mathrm dt.$$
- **Renewal root** ([source](#eq:aj)): The increasing function $\sum_{j\ge1}a_jz^j$ goes from zero to infinity on $0<z<1$, so there is a unique $\rho\in(0,1)$ such that $$\sum_{j\ge1}a_j\rho^j=1.$$
- **Renewal constants and recurrence** ([source](#eq:renewal)): Define $$\label{eq:renewal} \lambda=\log(1/\rho),\qquad \gamma=\left(\sum_{j\ge1}ja_j\rho^j\right)^{-1},\qquad g_0=1,\quad g_j=\sum_{d=1}^j a_dg_{j-d}.$$
- **Phase and dimension** ([source](#eq:scales)): For the scale used in our theorem, put $$\label{eq:scales} B=\log_2x,\qquad m=\left\lfloor\frac{\log B-\log_2B}{\lambda}\right\rfloor, \qquad \theta=\frac{\log B-\log_2B}{\lambda}-m,$$
- **Simplex volume scale** ([source](#eq:Gj)): so that $0\le\theta<1$, and write $$\label{eq:Gj} G_j=\frac{B^j}{j!\prod_{i=1}^j g_i}\quad(0\le j\le m),\qquad G_0=1.$$
- **Normal prime** ([source](#stmt-def-normal)): For $S\ge\mathrm e^\mathrm e$, a prime $p$ is $S$-normal if $$\Omega(p-1,1,S)\le2\log_2S$$ and, for every $S\le U<T\le p-1$, $$\left\|\Omega(p-1,U,T)-(\log_2T-\log_2U)\right\| <\sqrt{\log_2S\,\log_2T}.$$
- **Basic witness** ([source](#stmt-def-basic)): A *basic witness* is a choice of primes $p_0,\ldots,p_L$ and an integer $a\ge1$ satisfying $$\label{eq:basic} \begin{gathered} p_0\ge x^{0.9},\qquad 0.9\widetilde b_i\le\log_2p_i\le1.1\widetilde b_i \quad(1\le i\le L),\\ (u_1,\ldots,u_L)\in S_L(\boldsymbol\xi),\qquad P^+(a)\le p_L,\qquad \log a\le\exp(2\widetilde b_L). \end{gathered}$$ Here $u_0=B$, and $u_i=\log_2p_i$ for $i\ge1$.
- **Companion count and weight** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For a positive integer $k$, define $$\label{eq:fk} \begin{aligned} N_k(x)&=\#\{v\in\mathcal V:v\le x,\ kx<\ell(v)\le(k+1)x\},\\ f_k(r)&=\min\{1,(k+1)/r\}-\min\{1,k/r\}. \end{aligned}$$
- **Reciprocal mass** ([source](#eq:mass)): For any function $f:[1,\infty)\to[0,1]$, define $$M_f(x;H)= \sum_{(d,p_1,\ldots,p_R)\in\mathcal D_H(x)} \frac{f(\ell(d)/d)}{d\prod_{i=1}^R(p_i-1)}. \label{eq:mass}$$
- **Coordinate simplex** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For integers $1\le j\le m$, $u_0=B$, and $\beta_i\ge1$ for $0\le i<j$, put $\beta_j=1$ and define the simplex $$S_j(\boldsymbol\beta)= \left\{(u_1,\ldots,u_j): s_i:=\beta_i u_i-\sum_{r=i+1}^j a_{r-i}u_r\ge0 \quad(0\le i\le j)\right\}.$$ The terminal inequality is $u_j\ge0$. For $j=0$, the simplex consists of the empty tuple and has volume $G_0=1$.
- **Tail witness** ([source](#eq:tail-bands)): A *tail witness* is a choice $$\eta=((Q_h)_{P\le h<H},a)$$ of primes $Q_h$ and a positive integer $a$ with the following properties. Writing $v_h=\log_2Q_h$, we require $$\label{eq:tail-bands} \begin{aligned} 0.9\alpha_s h\rho^{-h}&\le v_h\le1.1\alpha_s h\rho^{-h} &&(P\le h<H),\\ \sum_{l=P}^{h-1}a_{h-l}v_l &\le(1+10^{-4}\mathrm e^{-h/40})v_h &&(P\le h<H),\\ P^+(a)&\le Q_P,\qquad \log a\le\exp(2\alpha_sP\rho^{-P}). \end{aligned}$$
- **Tail threshold** ([source](#eq:tail-D)): For $h\ge H$, define $$\label{eq:tail-D} D(h,\eta)=\sum_{l=P}^{H-1}a_{h-l}v_l.$$
- **Arithmetic coefficient** ([source](#eq:AH)): For a function $f:[1,\infty)\to[0,1]$, the coefficient is $$A_H(f;s)=\rho^{H(H-1)/2} \left(\frac\gamma{\alpha_s}\right)^H \sum_{\substack{d\in\mathcal V\\\mathcal W_{H,s}(d)\ne\varnothing}} \frac{f(\ell(d)/d)}d\, \Pr\left(\bigcup_{\eta\in\mathcal W_{H,s}(d)} \mathcal E_\eta\right).$$
- **Value map on shortened tuples** ([source](#sec:collisions)): Their value map is $$F(p_0,\ldots,p_R,d)=d\prod_{j=0}^R(p_j-1).$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-basic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-smooth-tail-2): Normal-structure estimates, simplex boxes and smooth-cofactor bounds. -> Coverage of every preimage outside the exceptional value set.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-discard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-discard): Thin slack shells and shifted-prime factor-count bounds. -> Negligible discarded tuple count.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-comparison): Layered factor allocation, uniform sieve bounds and smooth Euler products. -> Ordered collision comparison bound.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-collisions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-collisions): Cancel common primes, align largest factors and recover residual tuple factorizations. -> Negligible ordered pairs of distinct tuples with equal values.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-unique-prefix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-unique-prefix): Apply finite-map counting and the coverage condition on every preimage. -> Unique shortened tuples and least-preimage factorization outside the exceptional set.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-mass): Count the largest prime with common endpoint parameters. -> Both endpoint counts share a reciprocal mass.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-cor-fixed-scaling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-fixed-scaling): Subtract endpoint formulas using the common mass and a positive counting-scale lower bound. -> The fixed positive dilation limit.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-tail-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tail-limit): Translate slack simplexes, combine witness unions by inclusion–exclusion and take the prefix-volume limit. -> Uniform approximation by finite arithmetic coefficients.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coefficient-convergence-2): Compare coefficients along exact-phase sequences using the normalized distinct-value count. -> Uniform coefficient convergence and the explicit asymptotic equivalent.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-endpoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-endpoint): Count the permitted largest-prime interval with additive weight perturbation bounds. -> An additive weighted asymptotic mass comparison.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-thm-companion) / [proof](#pm-h-47): Combine weighted convergence, full-fiber seed propagation and ratio tightness. -> Positive or identically zero least-preimage interval counts.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **finite-map**: Coverage outside the exceptional set and ordered good-fiber collisions bound the cardinality discrepancy and permit a bijection after removing exceptional fibers.
- **fixed-scaling**: The ratio of distinct totient counts at a fixed dilation tends to that dilation.
- **coefficient**: The unweighted arithmetic approximants converge uniformly in phase to a positive bounded coefficient.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **variable-dilation**: Use fixed scaling for a dilation that changes with the argument. -> The stated limit does not control a varying dilation.
- **count-witnesses**: Replace distinct tuples by all arithmetic witnesses. -> Several witnesses can produce the same tuple and inflate the count.
- **finite-rate**: Use the asymptotic formula as a certified finite-input error bound. -> The limiting statement supplies no finite-input rate.
- **unique-fiber**: Infer uniqueness of the entire totient preimage from prefix uniqueness. -> Distinct arithmetic tails may still produce the same value.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite-map counting lemma supplies a finite construction of the exceptional image set and the remaining bijection. This implementation does not compute the asymptotic totient coefficient.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON batch of cases {w, f, g, e}; W=range(w), T=range(len(f)).
# PRECONDITIONS: Integer w in [0,8], at most 8 domain elements; valid map and subsets; W minus E covered.
# OUTPUTS: Per case INVALID or [cardinality difference, discarded size, ordered collisions, exceptional image size, exceptional preimage size, bijection].
# TIME: Linear per admitted case in domain plus codomain size, ignoring exact integer bit costs.
# SPACE: Linear per admitted case in domain plus codomain size.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integers and python-flint fmpz; no floating point.
# FAILURE: Invalid cases yield INVALID; malformed batches raise an error; no asymptotic certificate.
import json,sys
from collections import Counter
from flint import fmpz

def count(c):
    w,f,g,e=c['w'],c['f'],c['g'],c['e']
    if type(w)!=int or not 0<=w<=8 or len(f)>8 or any(type(v)!=int or not 0<=v<w for v in f):
        return 'INVALID'
    n=len(f)
    if len(set(g))!=len(g) or len(set(e))!=len(e) or any(type(i)!=int or not 0<=i<n for i in g) or any(type(v)!=int or not 0<=v<w for v in e):
        return 'INVALID'
    if not set(range(w)).difference(e).issubset(f):
        return 'INVALID'
    good=set(g); discarded=set(range(n))-good
    hist=Counter(f[i] for i in good)
    K=sum((fmpz(r)*fmpz(r-1) for r in hist.values()),fmpz(0))
    bad=set(e) | {f[i] for i in discarded} | {v for v,r in hist.items() if r>=2}
    pre=sum(v in bad for v in f)
    remaining=[v for v in f if v not in bad]
    bij=len(remaining)==len(set(remaining)) and set(remaining)==set(range(w))-bad
    return [n-w,len(discarded),int(K),len(bad),pre,bij]
print(json.dumps([count(c) for c in json.load(sys.stdin)],separators=(',',':')))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | An offline audit of deduplicated representations can use the finite-map inequalities when the complete domain and codomain are supplied, the value map is exact, and coverage outside the exceptional subset has been checked. This transfer concerns image counts and fiber multiplicities. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source studies arithmetic preimages and simplex volumes. It supplies no game-state transition model, simulation dynamics, or accuracy contract for a simulator. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A finite-map checker can report image-count discrepancies, discarded representations, collision multiplicities, and the subset on which the supplied map is bijective. Its contract requires explicit complete finite sets and checked coverage. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite-map counting lemma yields an exact finite audit procedure: group good elements by their images, count ordered collisions, and remove exceptional fibers before checking bijectivity. The transfer requires a total map and coverage outside the supplied exceptions. |
| data_structures | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A map from each value to its good and discarded preimages can preserve the multiplicities needed by the finite-map counting lemma. This is an inferred representation for exact finite audits; collapsing a fiber to a presence flag loses the information required by the bounds. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite arithmetic coefficient and shifted-prime estimates specify no accelerator execution model, memory layout, or hardware resource bound. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source counts distinct totient values and studies least preimages. It supplies no security model, key-distribution assumptions, or cryptanalytic cost bound. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source uses uniform convergence of arithmetic coefficients and a simplex probability measure. It supplies no learning problem, training procedure, or generalization bound. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The simplex estimates concern volumes and representation counts. The source does not specify an engineering objective, feasible decision model, or optimization algorithm. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The value-map collisions are collisions of arithmetic representations. The source supplies no communication, concurrency, failure, or consistency model for a distributed implementation. |
| other (analytic_number_theory) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The source establishes an explicit asymptotic equivalent for distinct totient values, fixed-factor scaling, and a weighted least-preimage dichotomy. The coefficient retains finite tail data, but its convergent series and limiting construction do not supply a practical finite-input approximation contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-fixed-scale** (hypothesis): The dilation limit concerns a fixed positive multiplier. It does not establish the same limit for an input-dependent multiplier. Blocked use: ['Extrapolating the dilation limit to a changing multiplier.']
- **L-order-limits** (hypothesis): The arithmetic tail cut is fixed before the input tends to infinity, and the cut grows afterwards. A simultaneous choice of cut and input requires a separate justification. Blocked use: ['Choosing the cut as a function of the input without proving uniform errors for that choice.']
- **L-finite-error** (approximation): The asymptotic equivalent does not itself give a certified error tolerance or an explicit input threshold for evaluating a finite totient count. Blocked use: ['Treating the asymptotic equivalent as an exact count.', 'Advertising a certified finite-input relative error from the limiting statement alone.']
- **L-phase-regularity** (unknown): Regularity of the phase coefficient is not asserted. Continuity, differentiability, and interpolation guarantees cannot be inferred from uniform convergence alone. Blocked use: ['Interpolating phase coefficients under an assumed continuity guarantee.']
- **L-prefix-not-fiber** (counterexample_scope): Uniqueness concerns the long prime prefix and its tail totient. Different arithmetic tails can still yield the same value. Blocked use: ['Inferring a unique complete totient preimage.', 'Counting witnesses instead of distinct tail values.']
- **L-seed-unknown** (unknown): The complete classification of positive least-preimage alternatives and the unboundedness of least-preimage ratios remain unresolved. Blocked use: ['Claiming positivity for every fixed positive integer from the companion formula alone.', 'Claiming a bound or an unboundedness theorem for all least-preimage ratios.']
- **L-zero-alternative** (edge_case): The companion coefficient and count vanish when no seed exceeds the stated least-preimage threshold. A positive multiplicative approximation therefore requires the positive seed alternative. Blocked use: ['Dividing by the companion coefficient without establishing positivity.']
- **L-weight-convergence** (hypothesis): The approximation applies to bounded nonnegative weights, but uniform coefficient convergence requires an input comparison independent of the tail cut. Arbitrary-weight convergence is not established merely by the approximation. Blocked use: ['Extending coefficient convergence to every weight without the common-comparison hypothesis.']
- **L-series-computation** (approximation): Finite arithmetic witness data do not make the exponential weights finite series. Numerical truncation requires a proved remainder bound, and finiteness alone supplies no practical resource guarantee. Blocked use: ['Treating a truncated exponential-weight series as the exact coefficient.', 'Inferring practical enumeration cost from finiteness of the tail.']
- **L-map-coverage** (hypothesis): The finite-map cardinality bounds require complete finite sets, a total map into the supplied codomain, a partition of the domain, and coverage outside the exceptional set. A partial sample does not establish these premises. Blocked use: ['Using sampled fibers as complete cardinality evidence.', 'Applying the bijection conclusion before checking coverage.']
- **L-product-squarefree** (hypothesis): The layered comparison requires squarefreeness of the entire common product above the last cutoff. Separate squarefreeness of each shifted prime factor is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Replacing the product squarefreeness hypothesis by separate checks on each shift.']
- **L-additive-boundary** (edge_case): The weighted endpoint estimate is additive. At a zero weight, it does not furnish a relative approximation, and endpoint perturbations must still be controlled. Blocked use: ['Treating a zero companion weight as supporting a relative-error estimate.']
- **L-finite-verification** (formal_scope): An exact finite-map implementation verifies the supplied finite instances. Such checks do not certify the analytic asymptotic equivalent, the sieve estimates, or the infinite coefficient limit. Blocked use: ['Presenting finite-map instance checks as verification of the full asymptotic theorem.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 024 (`lean/docs/024.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [An asymptotic formula for the number of totients](../../preprints/An-asymptotic-formula-for-the-number-of-totients-September-25-2026/An-asymptotic-formula-for-the-number-of-totients-September-25-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Let $V(x)$ count the distinct values of Euler's totient function up to $x$. The formalization constructs the paper's explicit positive main term from finite arithmetic approximants and proves that their ratio tends to one. In particular, $V(cx)/V(x)\to c$ for every fixed $c>0$, answering the Erdős–Hall regular-variation question.
>
> The formalization also gives asymptotics for totients $v\le x$ whose least preimage $\ell(v)=\min\{n\ge1:\varphi(n)=v\}$ lies between $kx$ and $(k+1)x$. The associated coefficient is positive under the stated existence condition; when no totient $d$ satisfies $kd<\ell(d)$, the count and coefficient are identically zero. The cases $k=1,2$ have positive coefficients.

Comparator source `TotientCompanionZero.lean` (3544 bytes, sha256 `6e6e993b423efc7a30384d656956721918f9c484fca64036169715106799c878`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! The no-seed alternative of the weighted totient companion theorem. -/

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators Topology
open Filter

namespace TotientAsymptotic

def IsTotient (v : ℕ) : Prop := ∃ n : ℕ, 0 < n ∧ n.totient = v

def ell (v : ℕ) : ℕ := by
  classical
  exact if h : IsTotient v then Nat.find h else 0

def a (j : ℕ) : ℝ := (j + 1 : ℝ) * Real.log (j + 1) - j * Real.log j - 1

def RenewalRootSet : Set ℝ :=
  {z | 0 < z ∧ z < 1 ∧ (∑' j : ℕ, a (j+1) * z^(j+1)) = 1}

def rho : ℝ := sInf RenewalRootSet

def lam : ℝ := Real.log (1 / rho)

def gamma : ℝ := (∑' j : ℕ, (j+1 : ℝ) * a (j+1) * rho^(j+1))⁻¹

def P (H : ℕ) : ℕ := ⌊Real.log (Real.log (H : ℝ))⌋₊
def alpha (s : ℝ) : ℝ := lam * Real.exp (lam * s)

def largestPrimeFactor (n : ℕ) : ℕ := max 1 (n.primeFactors.sup id)

structure TailDatum (H : ℕ) where
  Q : Fin H → ℕ
  cofactor : ℕ

def tailPrime {H : ℕ} (η : TailDatum H) (h : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  if hH : h < H then η.Q ⟨h, hH⟩ else 1

def tailLog {H : ℕ} (η : TailDatum H) (h : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  Real.log (Real.log (tailPrime η h : ℝ))

def IsWitness (H : ℕ) (s : ℝ) (η : TailDatum H) : Prop :=
  (∀ h : Fin H, h.val < P H → η.Q h = 1) ∧
  0 < η.cofactor ∧
  (∀ h ∈ Finset.Ico (P H) H, (tailPrime η h).Prime ∧
    (9 / 10 : ℝ) * alpha s * h * (rho^h)⁻¹ ≤ tailLog η h ∧
    tailLog η h ≤ (11 / 10 : ℝ) * alpha s * h * (rho^h)⁻¹ ∧
    (∑ l ∈ Finset.Ico (P H) h, a (h-l) * tailLog η l) ≤
      (1 + (1 / 10000 : ℝ) * Real.exp (-(h : ℝ) / 40)) * tailLog η h) ∧
  largestPrimeFactor η.cofactor ≤ tailPrime η (P H) ∧
  Real.log (η.cofactor : ℝ) ≤
    Real.exp (2 * alpha s * (P H : ℝ) * (rho^(P H))⁻¹)

def w {H : ℕ} (η : TailDatum H) : ℕ :=
  η.cofactor * ∏ h ∈ Finset.Ico (P H) H, tailPrime η h

def witnesses (H : ℕ) (s : ℝ) (d : ℕ) : Set (TailDatum H) :=
  {η | IsWitness H s η ∧ (w η).totient = d}

def D {H : ℕ} (h : ℕ) (η : TailDatum H) : ℝ :=
  ∑ l ∈ Finset.Ico (P H) H, a (h-l) * tailLog η l

def maxD {H : ℕ} (h : ℕ) (T : Finset (TailDatum H)) : ℝ := by
  classical
  exact if ht : T.Nonempty then T.sup' ht (D h) else 0

def AH (H : ℕ) (f : ℝ → ℝ) (s : ℝ) : ℝ := by
  classical
  exact rho^(H*(H-1)/2) * (gamma / alpha s)^H *
    ∑ᶠ d : ℕ,
      if IsTotient d then
        f ((ell d : ℝ) / d) / d *
          ∑ᶠ T : Finset (TailDatum H),
            if T.Nonempty ∧ (↑T : Set (TailDatum H)) ⊆ witnesses H s d then
              (-1 : ℝ)^(T.card-1) *
               Real.exp (-(gamma / alpha s) *
                 ∑' n : ℕ, rho^(H+n) * maxD (H+n) T)
            else 0
      else 0

def A (f : ℝ → ℝ) (s : ℝ) : ℝ := limUnder atTop (fun H : ℕ => AH H f s)

def N (k : ℕ) (x : ℝ) : ℝ := by
  classical
  exact (((Finset.Icc 1 ⌊x⌋₊).filter fun v =>
    IsTotient v ∧ (k : ℝ) * x < ell v ∧ (ell v : ℝ) ≤ (k+1 : ℝ) * x).card : ℝ)

def fk (k : ℕ) (r : ℝ) : ℝ := min 1 ((k+1 : ℝ) / r) - min 1 ((k : ℝ) / r)

/-- Without a totient value whose least preimage exceeds its prescribed multiple,
both the weighted count and its limiting coefficient vanish. -/
theorem companion_zero_case (k : ℕ) (_hk : 0 < k)
    (hno : ¬ ∃ d : ℕ, IsTotient d ∧ k*d < ell d) :
    (∀ x : ℝ, 0 < x → N k x = 0) ∧
    (∀ s ∈ Set.Ico (0 : ℝ) 1, A (fk k) s = 0) := by
  sorry

end TotientAsymptotic
end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let $`V(x)`$ count the distinct values of Euler's totient function up to <i>x</i>. We give an explicit asymptotic equivalent for $`V(x)`$. Its coefficient is a uniform limit of functions defined from finite arithmetic data. We also prove that $`V(cx)/V(x)\to c`$ as $`x\to\infty`$ for every fixed <i>c</i> &gt; 0, answering a question of Erdős and Hall.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Euler’s totient function $\varphi(n)$ counts the integers in $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ that are relatively prime to $n$. We write $$\mathcal V=\{\varphi(n):n\ge1\},\qquad
 V(x)=\#\{v\in\mathcal V:v\le x\}.$$ Counting distinct values requires more than counting integers with a prescribed factorization: different integers can have the same totient. This distinction is decisive in an asymptotic formula for $V(x)$.

Throughout the paper logarithms are natural, and $\log_j$ denotes the $j$-fold iterated logarithm.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### The counting problem.

Since $\varphi(p)=p-1$ for primes $p$, the prime number theorem gives $V(x)\ge\pi(x+1)\sim x/\log x$. Pillai proved that the totient values have density zero (Pillai 1929) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Pillai1929](#ref-Pillai1929)]. Erdős used the normal number of prime factors of $p-1$ to sharpen the upper bound to $V(x)\ll_\varepsilon x/(\log x)^{1-\varepsilon}$ for every $\varepsilon>0$ (Erdős 1935, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Erdos1935](#ref-Erdos1935)]. His later count of distinct values $\varphi(pr)$ with $p,r$ prime and $pr\le x$ gives the lower bound $(1+o(1))x\log_2x/\log x$ (Erdős 1945, 542–43) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Erdos1945](#ref-Erdos1945)]. Erdős and Hall obtained a much larger factor $\exp\{a(\log_3x)^2\}$ for every $0<a<1/\log16$ (Erdős and Hall 1976, 1–3) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ErdosHall1976](#ref-ErdosHall1976)]. Pomerance brought the upper bound onto the same logarithmic scale (Pomerance 1986, Equation (1.4)) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Pomerance1986](#ref-Pomerance1986)]. Maier and Pomerance then determined the leading constant (Maier and Pomerance 1988, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[MaierPomerance1988](#ref-MaierPomerance1988)]: $$V(x)=\frac{x}{\log x}
       \exp\{(C_0+o(1))(\log_3x)^2\},\qquad
 C_0=0.8178146\ldots.$$

<a id="eq:classical-order"></a>
In 1998, Ford refined this to an estimate with only a bounded multiplicative uncertainty (Ford 1998, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)]: $$\label{eq:classical-order}
 V(x)=\frac{x}{\log x}\exp\left\{
 \begin{aligned}
 &C_0(\log_3x-\log_4x)^2+D_0\log_3x\\
 &\hspace{12pt}-(D_0+\tfrac12-2C_0)\log_4x+O(1)
 \end{aligned}\right\},$$ where $D_0=2.1769687\ldots$; both constants are specified in the notation of Section <a href="#sec:statements" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:statements">2</a> below. Ford also described the typical prime factorizations of preimages (Ford 1998, Theorems 10 and 16) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)], which supply the structural starting point of our proof. All numbered references to his distribution paper use the revised arXiv version of 14 July 2013.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### Fixed dilation.

Erdős and Hall asked whether $$\frac{V(cx)}{V(x)}\longrightarrow c
 \qquad(c>1\text{ fixed})$$ in the final paragraph of (Erdős and Hall 1976, 3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[ErdosHall1976](#ref-ErdosHall1976)]. Erdős repeated the question in (Erdős 1979, 80) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Erdos1979](#ref-Erdos1979)]. Ford proved that $V(cx)-V(x)\asymp_c V(x)$ for every fixed $c>1$ (Ford 1998, Theorem 4) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)]. This controls the number of values in a fixed multiplicative interval, but neither it nor <a href="#eq:classical-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:classical-order">[eq:classical-order]</a> determines the limiting ratio.

We obtain an explicit asymptotic equivalent and prove the fixed-scale limit, thereby answering this question of Erdős and Hall positively. The coefficient in the equivalent retains finite arithmetic information. It is defined in Section <a href="#sec:statements" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:statements">2</a> by bounded prime and integer sums, inclusion–exclusion, and a uniformly convergent limit; its definition does not use $V$.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### The source of the coefficient.

Order the prime factors of a typical preimage from largest to smallest, and separate a long prefix from a shorter tail. Ford’s normal-structure estimates put the double logarithms of the large primes in a simplex. Most of that simplex can be counted by volume. Near its last coordinates the primes remain discrete, however, and several tail factorizations may give the same totient. We retain these tails exactly and take the volume of the union of the regions that they permit. Inclusion–exclusion of this finite union produces the arithmetic coefficient.

Erdős’s semiprime argument already controls collisions between products of shifted primes (Erdős 1945, Lemma 1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Erdos1945](#ref-Erdos1945)]. The quantitative shifted-prime normality and layered comparison used here were developed by Maier and Pomerance (Maier and Pomerance 1988, secs. 2–3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[MaierPomerance1988](#ref-MaierPomerance1988)] and Ford (Ford 1998, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)]. Here the comparison is proved under the hypotheses needed for the long prefix, with explicit costs for recovering the tuple data inside the residual factors. The resulting control of all ordered prefix collisions, combined with the exact finite-tail union, converts the volume into a count of distinct values. Counting the largest prime with common endpoint parameters first gives regular variation. Identifying the resulting mass with the arithmetic coefficient then gives the explicit equivalent. The errors are uniform in the phase; neither conclusion assumes continuity of the coefficient.

The prefix uniqueness used here is not uniqueness of the entire preimage: different arithmetic tails may still give the same value. A separate result on extreme complete fibers gives, for every fixed $\varepsilon>0$, infinitely many positive integers $v$ with $\#\{n\ge1:\varphi(n)=v\}>v^{1-\varepsilon}$ (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[OpenAITotientFibers2026](#ref-OpenAITotientFibers2026)]. That result concerns large individual fibers, rather than the asymptotic number of distinct values, and is not an input to the present proof.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Least preimages.

For $v\in\mathcal V$, let $$\ell(v)=\min\{n\ge1:\varphi(n)=v\}.$$ For each fixed positive integer $k$, the same passage of Erdős (Erdős 1979, 80) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Erdos1979](#ref-Erdos1979)] asks about the number of values $v\le x$ for which $kx<\ell(v)\le(k+1)x$. Section <a href="#sec:companion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:companion">7</a> gives a weighted asymptotic and proves that this count has order $V(x)$ precisely when some totient $d$ satisfies $\ell(d)>kd$; otherwise the count vanishes identically. The positive alternative holds for $k=1,2$. We do not determine which other integers $k$ satisfy this seed condition. In particular, the separate question whether $\ell(d)/d$ is unbounded, posed in (Erdős 1995, sec. I.9) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Erdos1995](#ref-Erdos1995)], remains unresolved here.

<a id="sec:statements"></a>
### The explicit main term

We first give the counting scale and the main theorem, then construct its arithmetic coefficient. Definitions involving $x$ are used only for sufficiently large $x$.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The scale

<a id="eq:aj"></a>
<a id="eq:renewal"></a>
<a id="eq:scales"></a>
<a id="eq:Gj"></a>
For $j\ge1$, put $$\label{eq:aj}
 a_j=(j+1)\log(j+1)-j\log j-1
     =\int_j^{j+1}\log t\,\mathrm dt.$$ These numbers are positive. The increasing function $\sum_{j\ge1}a_jz^j$ goes from zero to infinity on $0<z<1$, so there is a unique $\rho\in(0,1)$ such that $$\sum_{j\ge1}a_j\rho^j=1.$$ These coefficients and this root occur in Maier and Pomerance’s asymptotic estimate (Maier and Pomerance 1988, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[MaierPomerance1988](#ref-MaierPomerance1988)]. Define $$\label{eq:renewal}
 \lambda=\log(1/\rho),\qquad
 \gamma=\left(\sum_{j\ge1}ja_j\rho^j\right)^{-1},\qquad
 g_0=1,\quad g_j=\sum_{d=1}^j a_dg_{j-d}.$$ Our $\gamma$ is the constant denoted by $\lambda$ in Ford’s recurrence estimates (Ford 1998, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)]. Set $$C_0=\frac1{2\lambda},\qquad
 D_0=\frac{1+\log(\lambda/\gamma)}{\lambda}-\frac12;$$ these are the constants in the classical estimate <a href="#eq:classical-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:classical-order">[eq:classical-order]</a>. For the scale used in our theorem, put $$\label{eq:scales}
 B=\log_2x,\qquad
 m=\left\lfloor\frac{\log B-\log_2B}{\lambda}\right\rfloor,
 \qquad
 \theta=\frac{\log B-\log_2B}{\lambda}-m,$$ so that $0\le\theta<1$, and write $$\label{eq:Gj}
 G_j=\frac{B^j}{j!\prod_{i=1}^j g_i}\quad(0\le j\le m),\qquad G_0=1.$$ The factor $xG_m/\log x$ is Ford’s counting scale. The following theorem resolves its bounded multiplicative uncertainty by an explicitly constructed function of the phase $\theta$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For sufficiently large positive integers $H$, there are nonnegative functions $A_H(1;\cdot):[0,1)\to\mathbb R$, given by the finite arithmetic formula <a href="#eq:AH" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:AH">[eq:AH]</a> below, which converge uniformly as $H\to\infty$ to a function $A(1;\cdot)$ satisfying $$0<\inf_{0\le s<1}A(1;s)
   \le\sup_{0\le s<1}A(1;s)<\infty.$$ With the parameters <a href="#eq:renewal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:renewal">[eq:renewal]</a>–<a href="#eq:Gj" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:Gj">[eq:Gj]</a>, $$V(x)\sim\frac{x}{\log x}\,G_m A(1;\theta).$$ Moreover, for every fixed real $c>0$, $$\frac{V(cx)}{V(x)}\longrightarrow c.$$*

</div>

The first argument of $A$ records a weight; the main theorem uses the constant weight $1$. Its arithmetic construction below does not use $V$. The fixed-scale conclusion has a separate mechanism: it compares the same representation count at two endpoints. No regularity of $s\mapsto A(1;s)$ is asserted or needed.

<a id="subsec:coefficient"></a>
#### The arithmetic coefficient

The tail index runs in the opposite direction from the ordered prime index. In the proof we write a preimage as $p_0p_1\cdots p_La$, with the primes decreasing, and use two cuts $$R=m-H,\qquad L=m-P,\qquad P=\lfloor\log\log H\rfloor.$$ The long prefix ends at $p_R$, whereas the exact arithmetic tail contains $p_{R+1},\ldots,p_L$ and $a$. Thus the notation below is $Q_h=p_{m-h}$: its indices run from $P$ to $H-1$, and $Q_P$ is the smallest retained prime. For fixed $H$, these tail primes are bounded independently of $x$. We first let $x$ tend to infinity with $H$ fixed, and only afterwards let $H$ tend to infinity. The following definition uses the phase $s$ directly, without reference to $x$.

<a id="eq:tail-bands"></a>
<a id="eq:tail-D"></a>
Fix $s\in[0,1)$ and put $\alpha_s=\lambda\mathrm e^{\lambda s}$. Let $H$ be a sufficiently large positive integer and $P=\lfloor\log\log H\rfloor$. A *tail witness* is a choice $$\eta=((Q_h)_{P\le h<H},a)$$ of primes $Q_h$ and a positive integer $a$ with the following properties. Writing $v_h=\log_2Q_h$, we require $$\label{eq:tail-bands}
 \begin{aligned}
 0.9\alpha_s h\rho^{-h}&\le v_h\le1.1\alpha_s h\rho^{-h}
          &&(P\le h<H),\\
 \sum_{l=P}^{h-1}a_{h-l}v_l
   &\le(1+10^{-4}\mathrm e^{-h/40})v_h
          &&(P\le h<H),\\
 P^+(a)&\le Q_P,\qquad
 \log a\le\exp(2\alpha_sP\rho^{-P}).
 \end{aligned}$$ Here $P^+(a)$ is the largest prime factor of $a$, with $P^+(1)=1$; an empty sum is zero. Associate to $\eta$ the integer $$w(\eta)=a\prod_{h=P}^{H-1}Q_h.$$ For $d\in\mathcal V$, let $\mathcal W_{H,s}(d)$ be the set of tail witnesses for which $\varphi(w(\eta))=d$. For $h\ge H$, define $$\label{eq:tail-D}
 D(h,\eta)=\sum_{l=P}^{H-1}a_{h-l}v_l.$$ The set of all possible witnesses, even as $s$ varies, is finite for each fixed $H$: $\alpha_s$ lies between $\lambda$ and $\lambda/\rho$, and every prime and integer in <a href="#eq:tail-bands" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tail-bands">[eq:tail-bands]</a> is consequently bounded in terms of $H$. Also $D(h,\eta)=O_H(\log(h+1))$. Thus the series used below converges absolutely, uniformly over this finite set and over $s$.

<a id="eq:tail-events"></a>
<a id="eq:AH"></a>
A tail totient must contribute once even when it has several witnesses. The union that enforces this can be described by independent exponential random variables $E_h$ of mean one, indexed by $h\ge H$. For each witness put $$\label{eq:tail-events}
 \mathcal E_\eta=
 \bigcap_{h\ge H}
 \left\{E_h\ge\frac\gamma{\alpha_s}\rho^hD(h,\eta)\right\}.$$ For a function $f:[1,\infty)\to[0,1]$, the coefficient is $$A_H(f;s)=\rho^{H(H-1)/2}
       \left(\frac\gamma{\alpha_s}\right)^H
       \sum_{\substack{d\in\mathcal V\\\mathcal W_{H,s}(d)\ne\varnothing}}
       \frac{f(\ell(d)/d)}d\,
       \Pr\left(\bigcup_{\eta\in\mathcal W_{H,s}(d)}
                    \mathcal E_\eta\right).$$ An intersection of events requires each $E_h$ to exceed the largest of the corresponding thresholds. Independence and absolute convergence of their sum therefore turn finite inclusion–exclusion into the explicit expression $$\label{eq:AH}
\begin{split}
 A_H(f;s)={}&\rho^{H(H-1)/2}
       \left(\frac{\gamma}{\alpha_s}\right)^H
       \sum_{\substack{d\in\mathcal V\\\mathcal W_{H,s}(d)\ne\varnothing}}
       \frac{f(\ell(d)/d)}d\\[-2pt]
 &\quad\times
 \sum_{\varnothing\ne T\subseteq\mathcal W_{H,s}(d)}
       (-1)^{|T|-1}
 \exp\left\{-\frac{\gamma}{\alpha_s}
       \sum_{h=H}^{\infty}\rho^h
                    \max_{\eta\in T}D(h,\eta)\right\}.
\end{split}$$ For $f=1$, the factor involving $\ell$ is simply $1$: no least-preimage search is part of the main coefficient. For other weights, any witness with totient $d$ supplies the finite search bound $\ell(d)\le w(\eta)$. The probability expression also shows directly that $A_H(f;s)\ge0$. Section <a href="#sec:limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:limits">6</a> identifies these probabilities with limiting normalized prefix volumes: $1/d$ comes from counting the largest prime, and the prefactor is the limiting ratio of the prefix volume to $G_m$.

When the limit exists, write $$A(f;s)=\lim_{H\to\infty}A_H(f;s).$$ Each approximant uses finite arithmetic data and an absolutely convergent series, not an unspecified counting constant. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> justifies the limit for $f=1$; the next result does so for the weights needed to count least preimages. Convergence for arbitrary $f$ is not required.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Least-preimage intervals

For a positive integer $k$, define <a id="eq:fk"></a>$$\label{eq:fk}
 \begin{aligned}
 N_k(x)&=\#\{v\in\mathcal V:v\le x,\ kx<\ell(v)\le(k+1)x\},\\
 f_k(r)&=\min\{1,(k+1)/r\}-\min\{1,k/r\}.
 \end{aligned}$$ The function $f_k$ is nonnegative and bounded by $1$.

<a id="stmt-thm-companion"></a>
<div id="thm:companion" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2**. *For each fixed positive integer $k$, the functions $A_H(f_k;s)$ converge uniformly for $0\le s<1$, and $$N_k(x)=\frac{x}{\log x}\,G_m\bigl(A(f_k;\theta)+o(1)\bigr).$$ There are two possibilities.*

1.  *If some $d\in\mathcal V$ satisfies $\ell(d)>kd$, then $$\inf_{0\le s<1}A(f_k;s)>0,\qquad
      N_k(x)\sim\frac{x}{\log x}\,G_m A(f_k;\theta)
           \asymp_k V(x).$$*

2.  *If no such $d$ exists, then $N_k(x)=0$ for every $x>0$, and $A(f_k;s)=0$ for every $s\in[0,1)$.*

*The first alternative holds for $k=1,2$.*

</div>

This theorem does not determine which other integers $k$ satisfy the first alternative. Whether the first alternative holds for every positive integer $k$ is equivalent to the unboundedness of $\ell(d)/d$; the weighted formula does not settle this question.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Proof strategy

Two parts of the argument ensure that this construction counts values once. First, outside an exceptional set, *every* preimage has the required form. Second, collisions between different long prefixes have negligible total multiplicity. For the latter we use the layered shifted-prime method of Maier and Pomerance (Maier and Pomerance 1988, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[MaierPomerance1988](#ref-MaierPomerance1988)], in the form developed by Ford, and adapt his comparison (Ford 1998, Lemma 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)] to a different set of hypotheses, proving the necessary uniform bound in Section <a href="#sec:layers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:layers">4</a>. The number of layers grows, but each sieve application concerns only two or three linear forms. The comparison and the subsequent control of tuple multiplicities are the ingredients that turn a volume estimate into an asymptotic for distinct values.

The end of the continuous prefix and the end of the retained prime list are different cuts. Their separation makes the exceptional-set estimates small enough to absorb the smooth tail. Section <a href="#sec:extraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:extraction">3</a> obtains the required preimages directly from Ford’s Theorems 10 and 16 and develops the volume estimates. Section <a href="#sec:collisions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:collisions">5</a> proves prefix uniqueness and transfers tuple counts to distinct values. Section <a href="#sec:limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:limits">6</a> counts the largest prime using common endpoint parameters, obtaining fixed dilation before identifying the arithmetic limit. Finally, Section <a href="#sec:companion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:companion">7</a> weights that prime count to locate least preimages and proves the positive and zero alternatives.

<a id="sec:extraction"></a>
### Extracting a long prime prefix

Our first task is to represent almost every totient by a long prime prefix and a bounded arithmetic tail. The exceptional set must be small in a stronger sense: outside it, *every* preimage must have the required form. Later we shall count distinct prefix–tail tuples, and remove tuples that fail the conditions needed to compare two representations.

<a id="eq:cuts"></a>
<a id="eq:alpha-comparison"></a>
Throughout this section $H$ is a sufficiently large fixed integer, $P=\lfloor\log\log H\rfloor$, and $$\label{eq:cuts}
 R=m-H,\qquad L=m-P.$$ We first let $x\to\infty$, and then let $H\to\infty$. Write $h_i=m-i$ and $$\alpha=\frac{B\rho^m}{m},\qquad
 b_i=\alpha h_i\rho^{-h_i},\qquad
 \widetilde b_i=\alpha_\theta h_i\rho^{-h_i},\qquad
 \xi_i=1+10^{-4}\mathrm e^{-h_i/40}.$$ In particular $b_0=B$. The definitions in <a href="#eq:scales" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scales">[eq:scales]</a> give $$\label{eq:alpha-comparison}
 \frac{\alpha}{\alpha_\theta}
 =\frac{\log B}{\lambda m}
 =1+\frac{\lambda\theta+\log\log B}{\lambda m}
 =1+O\left(\frac{\log m}{m}\right).$$ Thus $\alpha$ and $\alpha_\theta$ stay in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$, uniformly in the phase. We shall only need the coarse bounds <a id="eq:rho-bounds"></a>$$\label{eq:rho-bounds}
 0.54<\rho<0.545,$$ which follow from the numerical value recorded in (Ford 1998, (1.4)) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)]. They imply $\lambda<1$, $18<30\lambda<20$, and $1.1\rho<0.6$. Together with <a href="#eq:alpha-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:alpha-comparison">[eq:alpha-comparison]</a>, these inequalities justify the fixed constants $0.61$ and $0.62$ used below. We use $$K_H=\exp(CP\rho^{-P})=H^{o(1)},$$ where the absolute constant $C$ may be enlarged from one upper bound to the next. The last equality follows from $\lambda=\log(1/\rho)<1$. The notation $\varepsilon_H$ denotes a positive quantity tending to zero with $H$, and $o_{x;H}(1)$ denotes a quantity tending to zero as $x\to\infty$ for fixed $H$. All such estimates below are uniform in the phase.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### Structure estimates from Ford

<a id="eq:ford-scale"></a>
<a id="eq:g-asymptotic"></a>
<a id="eq:G-uniform"></a>
Ford’s order estimate and fundamental-simplex estimates give $$\label{eq:ford-scale}
 V(x)\asymp \frac{x}{\log x}G_m,$$ and his recurrence estimate gives $$\label{eq:g-asymptotic}
 g_j=\gamma\rho^{-j}+O(1),\qquad g_j\asymp\rho^{-j}.$$ See (Ford 1998, Theorem 1, Lemmas 3.4 and 3.7, and Corollary 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)]. Ford denotes our constant $\gamma$ by $\lambda$. In particular, for $0\le j\le m$, $$\label{eq:G-uniform}
 \frac{G_j}{G_m}
 =\prod_{i=j+1}^m\frac{ig_i}{B}
 \le C^{m-j}\rho^{(m-j)(m-j-1)/2}\ll1.$$

<a id="eq:ford-bands"></a>
We shall use two quantitative structure results of Ford. Order the prime factors of a preimage $n$ with repetitions as $p_0\ge p_1\ge\cdots$. For each fixed $0<\eta\le1/8$, his Theorem 10, together with the missing-prime exception in Theorem 16, implies $$\label{eq:ford-bands}
 \#\left\{v\le x:\begin{array}{l}
 v\in\mathcal V,\ \text{some preimage has}\\
 |\log_2p_i/b_i-1|>\eta\text{ for some }1\le i\le m-P
 \end{array}\right\}
 \ll_\eta V(x)\mathrm e^{-c_\eta P}.$$ Here and below a missing indicated prime is a failure of the condition. For completeness, the summation giving <a href="#eq:ford-bands" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ford-bands">[eq:ford-bands]</a> is short. For $i\le3\eta m$, Theorem 10(b) contributes at most $Cm\mathrm e^{-\eta m/13}V(x)$. For $i>3\eta m$, Theorem 10(a) contributes at most $$C V(x)\frac{i}{\eta m}
 \exp\left\{-\frac{\eta^2m(m-i)}{4i}\right\}
 \le \frac{CV(x)}{\eta}\mathrm e^{-\eta^2(m-i)/4}.$$ Summing over $m-i\ge P$ proves the assertion for sufficiently large $x$ when the indicated primes exist. A missing $p_L$ is included in the $O(V(x)\mathrm e^{-P^2/4})$ exceptional set of Theorem 16, which is absorbed in the displayed bound. The convention preceding Ford’s Theorem 10 counts values with *some* failing preimage; hence the complement of this set controls every preimage.

<a id="eq:ford-simplex"></a>
Ford’s Theorem 16, with its parameter $\Psi=P$, states that all but $O(V(x)\mathrm e^{-P^2/4})$ values have every preimage satisfying $$\label{eq:ford-simplex}
 \log_2p_L>2,\qquad
 \sum_{r=i+1}^L a_{r-i}u_r\le\xi_i u_i\quad(0\le i<L),
 \qquad u_0=B,\quad u_i=\log_2p_i\ (i\ge1).$$ Its final adjacent-coordinate inequality has coefficient $1$ in place of $a_1<1$; weakening that inequality gives <a href="#eq:ford-simplex" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ford-simplex">[eq:ford-simplex]</a>.

Write $\Omega(n,U,T)$ for the number of prime factors of $n$ belonging to $(U,T]$, counted with multiplicity, and put $\Omega(n)=\Omega(n,1,n)$, including $\Omega(1)=0$.

<a id="stmt-def-normal"></a>
<div id="def:normal" class="definition">

**Definition 3**. For $S\ge\mathrm e^\mathrm e$, a prime $p$ is $S$-normal if $$\Omega(p-1,1,S)\le2\log_2S$$ and, for every $S\le U<T\le p-1$, $$\left|\Omega(p-1,U,T)-(\log_2T-\log_2U)\right|
 <\sqrt{\log_2S\,\log_2T}.$$

</div>

<a id="eq:normal-primes"></a>
We use Definition 1 in the 2013 revision (Ford 1998) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)]. Its Lemma 2.6 gives, uniformly for $z\ge3$, $$\label{eq:normal-primes}
 \#\{p\le z:p\text{ is not }S\text{-normal}\}
 \ll \frac{z}{\log z}(\log_2z)^5(\log S)^{-1/6}.$$ The function $\Omega$ in these statements counts prime factors with multiplicity.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### Basic witnesses and the coverage target

We now specify the representations that these structure estimates will provide. The simplex notation records the inequalities on their prime coordinates; the bounds on the last factor will make the arithmetic tail finite for fixed $H$.

For integers $1\le j\le m$, $u_0=B$, and $\beta_i\ge1$ for $0\le i<j$, put $\beta_j=1$ and define the simplex $$S_j(\boldsymbol\beta)=
 \left\{(u_1,\ldots,u_j):
 s_i:=\beta_i u_i-\sum_{r=i+1}^j a_{r-i}u_r\ge0
 \quad(0\le i\le j)\right\}.$$ The terminal inequality is $u_j\ge0$. For $j=0$, the simplex consists of the empty tuple and has volume $G_0=1$.

<a id="stmt-def-basic"></a>
<div id="def:basic" class="definition">

<a id="eq:basic"></a>
**Definition 4**. A *basic witness* is a choice of primes $p_0,\ldots,p_L$ and an integer $a\ge1$ satisfying $$\label{eq:basic}
 \begin{gathered}
 p_0\ge x^{0.9},\qquad
 0.9\widetilde b_i\le\log_2p_i\le1.1\widetilde b_i
 \quad(1\le i\le L),\\
 (u_1,\ldots,u_L)\in S_L(\boldsymbol\xi),\qquad
 P^+(a)\le p_L,\qquad \log a\le\exp(2\widetilde b_L).
 \end{gathered}$$ Here $u_0=B$, and $u_i=\log_2p_i$ for $i\ge1$.

</div>

For our parameter range the prime bands are disjoint and decreasing; also $p_0>p_1$. Thus the displayed prime prefix is strictly decreasing. Repetitions of $p_L$ inside $a$ are allowed.

<figure id="fig:two-cuts">

<figcaption>The two cuts in a basic witness. We count $p_{0}$ by the prime number theorem and approximate the $p_{1}, …, p_{R}$ coordinates by volume. The later primes and the smooth integer $a$ remain discrete.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-two-cuts"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:two-cuts`)

```latex
 \node at (3.2,0.45) {$p_1\ \cdots\ p_R$};
 \node at (8,0.45) {$p_{R+1}\ \cdots\ p_L$};
 \node at (11.5,0.45) {$a$};
 \draw[dashed] (5.55,-0.1)--(5.55,1.05);
 \draw[dashed] (10.45,-0.1)--(10.45,1.05);
 \node[above] at (5.55,1.05) {$R=m-H$};
 \node[above] at (10.45,1.05) {$L=m-P$};
 \draw[decorate,decoration={brace,mirror,amplitude=4pt}]
       (0,-0.1)--(5.2,-0.1);
 \draw[decorate,decoration={brace,mirror,amplitude=4pt}]
       (5.9,-0.1)--(11.9,-0.1);
 \node[below] at (2.6,-0.35) {prefix};
 \node[below] at (8.9,-0.35) {finite arithmetic tail};
\end{tikzpicture}
```

Visual witness description of PDF page 9 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
Horizontal schematic diagram illustrating the partitioning of the components of a basic witness: $p_0 \quad p_1 \cdots p_R \quad p_{R+1} \cdots p_L \quad a$. Two vertical dashed cut lines extend downward from labels above: the first cut is labeled "$R = m - H$" between $p_R$ and $p_{R+1}$; the second cut is labeled "$L = m - P$" between $p_L$ and $a$. Below the sequence, horizontal underbraces designate groupings: an underbrace under $p_1 \cdots p_R$ is labeled "prefix", and an underbrace spanning under $p_{R+1} \cdots p_L \quad a$ is labeled "finite arithmetic tail". The prime $p_0$ sits at the far left before the prefix.
```


<a id="stmt-prop-basic"></a>
<div id="prop:basic" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:cofactor-size"></a>
**Proposition 5**. *There is a set $E_x\subseteq\mathcal V\cap[1,x]$ with $$|E_x|\le\{\varepsilon_H+o_{x;H}(1)\}V(x)$$ such that every preimage of every $v\in(\mathcal V\cap[1,x])\setminus E_x$ is $p_0\cdots p_La$ for a basic witness. One may take $$\varepsilon_H\ll
 \mathrm e^{-cP}+K_H\exp\{-\exp(cP\rho^{-P})\}.$$ For every basic witness, putting $w=p_{R+1}\cdots p_La$, we have $$\label{eq:cofactor-size}
 \begin{gathered}
 p_1\cdots p_La\le\exp((\log x)^{0.8}),\\
 \log w\le H\exp(0.62b_R)+\exp(2\widetilde b_L),
 \qquad w<p_R.
 \end{gathered}$$ In particular $w$ is bounded in terms of $H$ alone, uniformly in the phase.*

</div>

We prove the proposition after the volume and smooth-cofactor estimates below. Those estimates also prepare a second task: we shall retain the shortened data $(p_0,\ldots,p_R,\varphi(w))$, then delete those that fail the strict inequalities and normality conditions needed for the collision argument. Coverage concerns *values*; this later deletion must count the distinct shortened tuples, including all tuples that represent the same value.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### Simplex volume and concentration

The coordinate scaling follows Ford’s fundamental-simplex method (Ford 1998, Lemma 3.2 and Corollary 3.3) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)]. Our last adjacent coefficient is $a_1$ rather than $1$, so the exact volume is slightly different from his; we derive it from the slacks below. The recurrence estimate <a href="#eq:g-asymptotic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:g-asymptotic">[eq:g-asymptotic]</a> is Ford’s Lemma 3.7; his Remark 1 credits its streamlined proof to Lau’s solution of a recurrence problem (Ford and Lau 2000) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[FordLau2000](#ref-FordLau2000)].

The next estimates turn sums over the allowed prime coordinates into simplex volumes. We also bound the mass near a coordinate boundary or a simplex face, so that removing these regions has a controlled cost.

<a id="stmt-lem-simplex"></a>
<div id="lem:simplex" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *Let $1\le j\le m$. The true simplex $S_j(\boldsymbol1)$ has volume $G_j$. Under its uniform volume measure the variables $W_i=g_is_i/B$ are uniform on $\{W_i\ge0:\sum_{i=0}^jW_i=1\}$. Moreover, $$\operatorname{Vol}S_j(\boldsymbol\beta)
 \le G_j\prod_{r<j}\beta_r^{j-r}.$$ Within $S_j(\boldsymbol\beta)$, a specified slack $s_t$ in an interval of length $D$ occupies at most a fraction $jg_tD/B$ of the volume. A specified coordinate $u_t$, $1\le t\le j$, in such an interval occupies at most $jg_t\beta_tD/B$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-simplex"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-simplex</summary>

*Proof.* For the true simplex the triangular inversion is $$u_i=\sum_{t=i}^j g_{t-i}s_t,\qquad
 B=\sum_{t=0}^j g_ts_t.$$ The transformation from $u_1,\ldots,u_j$ to $s_1,\ldots,s_j$ has determinant $1$. This proves the volume and distribution statements.

Set $K_i=\prod_{r<i}\beta_r$, $K_0=1$. The map $u_i\mapsto u_i/K_i$ sends $S_j(\boldsymbol\beta)$ into $S_j(\boldsymbol1)$, because $K_r\ge\beta_iK_i$ when $r>i$. Its inverse Jacobian is $\prod_{r<j}\beta_r^{j-r}$.

For the slice estimates, define $\gamma'_0=1$ and $\gamma'_t=\beta_t^{-1}\sum_{i<t}\gamma'_ia_{t-i}$. Then $0<\gamma'_t\le g_t$ and $$\sum_{t=0}^j\gamma'_ts_t=\beta_0B.$$ The marginal density of $s_t$, under the uniform measure on this simplex, is at most $j\gamma'_t/(\beta_0B)$. For the coordinate assertion, fix all other positive-index slacks. The coordinate $u_t$ is affine in $s_t$ with slope $1/\beta_t$. Integrating first in this fiber and bounding by the full projection volume gives the additional factor $\beta_t$. ◻

</details>

The following concentration estimate will be used in Section <a href="#sec:limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:limits">6</a> to remove the prefix bands after replacing prime sums by volumes. Write $z_+=\max\{z,0\}$.

<a id="stmt-lem-concentration"></a>
<div id="lem:concentration" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7**. *Fix $\eta>0$. There are constants $C_\eta,c_\eta>0$ such that, if $Q$ is an integer with $0\le Q<m$, $j=m-Q$, $1\le i\le j$, and $h_i\ge C_\eta(Q+1)$, then uniform volume measure on $S_j(\boldsymbol1)$ satisfies $$\Pr(|u_i/b_i-1|>\eta)\le C_\eta\mathrm e^{-c_\eta h_i}.$$ Also, for $T\ge0$, $$\Pr(u_j\ge T)=(1-g_jT/B)_+^j\le\mathrm e^{-jg_jT/B}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-concentration"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-concentration</summary>

*Proof.* By <a href="#eq:g-asymptotic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:g-asymptotic">[eq:g-asymptotic]</a> and Lemma <a href="#lem:simplex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:simplex">6</a>, $$\frac{u_i}{B\rho^i}
 =\sum_{t=i}^j(1+O(\rho^{t-i}))W_t.$$ Represent $W_t=E_t/\sum_{r=0}^jE_r$, where the $E_t$ are independent mean-one exponential variables. The main numerator has $j-i+1=h_i-Q+1$ terms, and $$\frac{(j-i+1)/(j+1)}{h_i/m}
 =1+O((Q+1)/h_i).$$ Exponential moment bounds give exponentially small probabilities for a fixed relative deviation of either the numerator or the denominator. The error numerator is bounded in absolute value by $C\sum_{r\ge0}\rho^rE_{i+r}$; this sum has a uniformly bounded exponential moment, since $\prod_{r\ge0}(1-z\rho^r)^{-1}<\infty$ for $0<z<1$. Once $h_i\ge C_\eta(Q+1)$, these three bounds give the first assertion. The second is the exact marginal tail of $W_j$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-16"></a>
#### Prime boxes and smooth cofactors

The use of reciprocal prime sums and enlarged coordinate boxes follows Ford’s Lemma 3.1 and the thickening arguments of his Section 3 (Ford 1998) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[FordTotients2013](#ref-FordTotients2013)]. We record the quantitative bounds for our simplexes, including the boundary errors needed for the tail unions.

<a id="eq:mertens-box"></a>
We use unit boxes in the coordinates $\log_2p_i$. Mertens’ theorem gives $$\label{eq:mertens-box}
 \sum_{b\le\log_2p<b+1}\frac1{p-1}=1+O(\mathrm e^{-b})
 \qquad(b\ge2).$$ The replacement of $1/p$ by $1/(p-1)$ changes the sum by $O(\exp(-\exp b))$.
